## db9862e

Added the trusted-backend launcher, pre-load contextual gate, JSON decision mode, and tests. A review found and fixed an initial bug where JSON inspection still launched the backend.

## Current limitation

The launcher delegates conversation formatting to the existing llama.cpp runtime. The first integrated interactive run showed unexpected transcript behavior; no multi-turn correctness claim is accepted until a deterministic harness passes.

## 2026-10-22 — Persistent session harness bounded

Added `benchmarks/persistent_session.py`, which keeps one backend process alive,
sends a fixed turn sequence through a PTY, and records command, requested/sent
turns, exit code, timeout, elapsed time, raw combined output, and SHA-256. Its
protocol tests use a deterministic two-turn fake backend.

The real Qwen2.5/Cortex `llama-cli` probe was bounded to 8 seconds and sent both
turns, but exited with code 255 and `input is empty` before producing a reply.
This is a backend interaction blocker; no persistent transcript equivalence or
state-reuse claim is accepted.

## 2026-10-22 — Readiness protocol repair

The local backend requires a conversation system prompt and emits a `> ` input
marker before accepting a turn. `persistent_session.py` now supplies the
system prompt and can wait for that marker before each turn. The fake-backend
tests remain passing, including a test that gates each turn on the marker. A
30-second real Qwen2.5 probe loaded the model and sent both turns through one
process, but timed out before an assistant reply (`returncode=-9`); equivalence
and state reuse remain open.

## 2026-10-22 — Bounded follow-up hardening

The session record now includes `ready_markers_seen`, and readiness waiting
observes backend exit so an early-exiting process cannot be reported only as a
readiness timeout. A 20-second probe with the local Qwen2.5 Coder 3B model
loaded successfully and delivered both turns, but timed out without a reply;
the multi-turn milestone remains rejected.

## 2026-10-22 — Sequential readiness consumption

Added a bounded marker reader that advances past each consumed readiness
marker, preventing repeated counting of an earlier `> ` occurrence in the same
PTY buffer. Added a regression test using a backend banner containing the
marker; it sends only the first turn and records the timeout.

## 2026-10-22 — Deterministic harness cycle closure

The persistent-session implementation is verified within its bounded scope:
one backend process, ordered readiness-marker consumption, requested/sent turn
accounting, timeout/exit reporting, and output hashing. No model artifacts or
generated binaries were added. The available real backend still does not
complete a reply within the bounded probe, so implementation work stops at the
reproducible harness boundary.

## 2026-10-22 — Direct probe closure

The direct diagnostic used the existing harness without source changes. The
1.2B LFM artifact is unsupported by the phase8 backend; the compatible Qwen2.5
Coder 3B artifact loaded and received its turn, but no reply arrived within
the 15-second bound. Backend generation remains the blocker.

## 2026-10-22 — Bounded cycle verification

The complete bounded suite passed 19 tests after the sequential
readiness-consumption repair. Work remains limited to the persistent-session
harness and deterministic fake-backend tests; no runtime or Cortex source was
changed. The real backend blocker remains an explicit rejection.

## 2026-10-22 — Cycle handoff verification

Re-ran the complete 19-test suite, `git diff --check`, and JSON parsing for the
durable autonomy state. No implementation changes were needed after the
readiness-consumption repair. The verified source remains bounded to the
persistent-session harness; no model artifacts, checkpoints, credentials, or
generated binaries were added.
## 2026-10-22 — Bounded engineering cycle

Revalidated the existing harness implementation without expanding runtime
scope: its command builder supplies the conversation system prompt, and its
marker reader consumes each readiness occurrence exactly once. The 19-test
suite passed; no Cortex source or model-side asset was changed.

No source expansion was required. Re-ran the 19-test suite and structural
